```php title="Controller"
$file->moveFile('path/to/foo.txt', 'path/to/bar.txt');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/output ---


# File::output

> Send a file to the browser

## Description

```php
public bool File::output( string $fileName )
```

Prints the contents of `$fileName` with `readfile()`, a piece at a time, so a file of any size can be sent ([File::readFile](./readfile.mdx) reads it all into memory). Headers are not sent: send them before. Returns `false` when `$fileName` is not a file or cannot be read.

For files uploaded through the CMS, use `Fegg\Http\Download`, which also sends the headers.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks      |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------------ |
| $fileName | string    |         | File to send |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$path = FEGG_SITE_DIR . '/data/export/list.csv';

header('Content-Type: text/csv; charset=UTF-8');
header('Content-Length: ' . filesize($path));

$file = new File();
$file->output($path);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/readfile ---


# File::readFile

> Read a file and return its contents

## Description

```php
public string File::readFile( string $fileName )
```

Returns the contents of `$fileName`, or an empty string when it is not a file or cannot be read. The whole file is read into memory: send a large file to the browser with [File::output](./output.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks      |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------------ |
| $fileName | string    |         | File to read |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$contents = $file->readFile('path/to/foo.txt');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/removedirectory ---


# File::removeDirectory

> Remove a directory with all its contents

## Description

```php
public bool File::removeDirectory( string $directory )
```

Deletes every file and subdirectory under `$directory` recursively, then removes `$directory` itself.

Symbolic links are never followed: a link inside the directory is removed as a link and what it points to is left alone. When `$directory` itself is a link, only the link is removed.

Returns `false` when `$directory` is not a directory or something could not be removed.

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks             |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------- |
| $directory | string    |         | Directory to remove |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->removeDirectory($this->config['upload_dir']['path'] . '/image/' . $id . '/');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/removeexpiredfile ---


# File::removeExpiredFile

> Remove the files older than the specified minutes

## Description

```php
public bool File::removeExpiredFile( string $path, int $min )
```

Deletes the files directly under `$path` whose modification time is `$min` minutes ago or older. Subdirectories are not touched.

Returns `true` when every expired file was removed (also when there was none), `false` when `$path` is not a directory or a file could not be removed.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                          |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| $path | string    |         | Target directory                                 |
| $min  | int       |         | Age in minutes after which a file is deleted     |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();

// Delete temporary files older than one day
$file->removeExpiredFile('path/to/tmp', 60 * 24);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/removefile ---


# File::removeFile

> Remove files

## Description

```php
public bool File::removeFile( string $file )
```

Deletes the file `$file`. When there is no file of that exact name, `$file` is used as a `glob()` pattern (`*`, `?`, `[...]`) and every matching file is deleted, so a file whose name contains `[` or `*` is removed itself, not the files the pattern would match. Directories are not removed.

Returns `false` when a file could not be removed. A pattern that matches nothing returns `true`.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------------- |
| $file | string    |         | File path or `glob()` pattern          |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->removeFile('path/to/foo.txt');

// Remove all the log files
$file->removeFile('path/to/log/*.log');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/touchdirectory ---


# File::touchDirectory

> Change the modification time of the files in a directory

## Description

```php
public bool File::touchDirectory( string $directory, string $datetime )
```

Sets the modification time of every file and subdirectory under `$directory` (recursively) to `$datetime`, parsed with `strtotime()`. Symbolic links are skipped.

Returns `false` when `$directory` is not a directory, `$datetime` cannot be parsed or a time could not be changed.

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks                                |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------------- |
| $directory | string    |         | Target directory                       |
| $datetime  | string    |         | Date and time (any `strtotime()` format) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->touchDirectory('path/to/foo', '2024-04-01 10:00:00');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/writefile ---


# File::writeFile

> Write data to a file

## Description

```php
public bool File::writeFile( string $file, string $data, [ string $writeOption = 'a' ] )
```

Opens `$file` with the `fopen()` mode `$writeOption`, writes `$data` and sets the permission of the file to `0644` (readable by everyone). The default mode `a` appends to the file; use `w` to overwrite it.

Returns `false` when the file cannot be opened or written.

## Arguments

| Name         | Data type | Default | Remarks                                    |
| ------------ | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| $file        | string    |         | File to write                              |
| $data        | string    |         | Data to write                              |
| $writeOption | string    | 'a'     | `fopen()` mode (`a`: append, `w`: overwrite) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();

// Append a line
$file->writeFile('path/to/foo.log', date('Y-m-d H:i:s') . " done\n");

// Overwrite the file
if (!$file->writeFile('path/to/foo.txt', 'New contents', 'w')) {
    // could not be written
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/http-client ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Requests to other services over HTTP

`Fegg\Http\Client` is a small HTTP client for calling other services: an API, a webhook, a feed. Fegg itself uses it for the [captcha](../captcha/index.mdx) checks, [RSS](../rss/index.mdx) and `fegg-cli doctor`.

- Every request has a timeout (10 seconds for the whole request and 3 to connect, by default).
- Only `http://` and `https://` URLs are requested.
- Redirects are not followed: a `301` or `302` comes back as it is.
- It uses cURL when the extension is installed, otherwise PHP's http stream wrapper. Nothing needs to be configured.

## How to use

Import `Fegg\Http\Client`, create it with `new` and call [get()](./methods/get.mdx), [post()](./methods/post.mdx) or [request()](./methods/request.mdx). Each returns an array:

| Key    | Data type | Remarks |
| :----- | :-------- | :------ |
| status | int       | HTTP status (`200`, `404` ...) |
| body   | string    | Response body |

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Http\Client;
use Fegg\Http\ClientException;
use Fegg\Log;

class Items extends Controller
{
    public function index()
    {
        $items = array();
        try {
            $response = (new Client(5))->get('https://api.example.com/items?category=2', array('Accept: application/json'));
            if ($response['status'] === 200) {
                $items = json_decode($response['body'], true) ?: array();
            } else {
                Log::warning('Items API answered {status}', array('status' => $response['status']));
            }
        } catch (ClientException $e) {
            Log::warning('Items API not reachable: {message}', array('message' => $e->getMessage()));
        }

        $this->page['items'] = $items;
        $this->displayPage('items');
    }
}
```

## Errors

- A response with an error status (`404`, `500` ...) is returned normally: check `status`.
- Only a transport failure (no connection, DNS, TLS, timeout) throws `Fegg\Http\ClientException` (a `\RuntimeException`). The message names the method, the URL and the cause.
- A URL that is not `http` or `https` throws `\InvalidArgumentException` before anything is sent.

The page should still work when the other service does not: catch `ClientException`, write it to the [log](../log/index.mdx) and show the page without that part. Keep the timeout short for a call made while a visitor waits.

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/http-client/methods/construct ---


# Client::__construct

> Create a client with its timeouts

## Description

```php
public Client::__construct( [ float $timeout = 10 [, float $connectTimeout = 3 [, ?string $transport = null ] ] ] )
```

The timeouts apply to every request made with this client.

## Arguments

| Name            | Data type    | Default | Remarks |
| --------------- | ------------ | ------- | ------- |
| $timeout        | float        | 10      | Seconds for the whole request |
| $connectTimeout | float        | 3       | Seconds to connect (cURL only; the stream wrapper has the whole `$timeout`) |
| $transport      | string\|null | `null`  | `'curl'` or `'stream'`. `null`: cURL when the extension is installed, otherwise the stream wrapper. Set it only in tests |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Http\Client;

$client = new Client();       // 10 seconds, 3 to connect
$quick  = new Client(3, 1);   // while a visitor waits for the page
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/http-client/methods/get ---


# Client::get

> Send a GET request

## Description

```php
public array Client::get( string $url [, array $headers = array() ] )
```

Returns `array('status' => int, 'body' => string)`. A response with an error status is returned normally; a transport failure throws `Fegg\Http\ClientException` (see [Errors](../index.mdx#errors)).

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default   | Remarks |
| -------- | --------- | --------- | ------- |
| $url     | string    |           | `http://` or `https://` URL, with its query string |
| $headers | array     | `array()` | Request headers, each as `'Name: value'` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Http\Client;

$query = http_build_query(array('category' => 2, 'q' => $this->in('q')));
$response = (new Client())->get('https://api.example.com/items?' . $query, array(
    'Accept: application/json',
    'Authorization: Bearer ' . feggEnv('ITEMS_API_TOKEN'),
));

if ($response['status'] === 200) {
    $items = json_decode($response['body'], true);
}
```

Keep tokens out of the code: read them with `feggEnv()` from `env.php` (see [Settings](../../../first/config/environments.mdx)).


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/http-client/methods/post ---


# Client::post

> Send a POST request

## Description

```php
public array Client::post( string $url, array|string $body [, array $headers = array() ] )
```

An array `$body` is sent as a form (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, the `Content-Type` is added). A string is sent as it is: add its `Content-Type` yourself, for JSON for example.

Returns `array('status' => int, 'body' => string)`. A transport failure throws `Fegg\Http\ClientException` (see [Errors](../index.mdx#errors)).

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type     | Default   | Remarks |
| -------- | ------------- | --------- | ------- |
| $url     | string        |           | `http://` or `https://` URL |
| $body    | array\|string |           | Form fields, or the body as it is |
| $headers | array         | `array()` | Request headers, each as `'Name: value'` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Http\Client;

$client = new Client();

// A form
$response = $client->post('https://example.com/api/entries', array('name' => $name, 'mail' => $mail));

// JSON
$response = $client->post(
    'https://hooks.example.com/notify',
    json_encode(array('text' => 'New entry from ' . $name)),
    array('Content-Type: application/json')
);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/http-client/methods/request ---


# Client::request

> Send a request with any method

## Description

```php
public array Client::request( string $method, string $url [, ?string $body = null [, array $headers = array() ] ] )
```

For methods other than GET and POST (`PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`, `HEAD` ...). The body is sent as it is.

Returns `array('status' => int, 'body' => string)`. A transport failure throws `Fegg\Http\ClientException`; a URL that is not `http` or `https` throws `\InvalidArgumentException` (see [Errors](../index.mdx#errors)).

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type    | Default   | Remarks |
| -------- | ------------ | --------- | ------- |
| $method  | string       |           | HTTP method |
| $url     | string       |           | `http://` or `https://` URL |
| $body    | string\|null | `null`    | Body, sent as it is |
| $headers | array        | `array()` | Request headers, each as `'Name: value'` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Http\Client;

$client = new Client();

$response = $client->request('PUT', 'https://api.example.com/items/42', json_encode($item), array(
    'Content-Type: application/json',
));

$response = $client->request('DELETE', 'https://api.example.com/items/42');
if ($response['status'] !== 204) {
    // ...
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/json-ld ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Structured data (schema.org JSON-LD) for search engines

`Fegg\Tool\JsonLd` builds [schema.org](https://schema.org/) structured data: the site's name and organization, breadcrumbs and articles, which search engines can show in their results. The methods return arrays; the template writes them as JSON in a `<script type="application/ld+json">` element.

## How to use

The methods are static. A controller sets the data as the site information `json_ld` with [setSiteinfo](../../basic/templates/helpers/setsiteinfo.mdx), usually several items in one [graph()](./methods/graph.mdx):

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/src/Controller/News.php"
use Fegg\Tool\JsonLd;

public function detail($id = '')
{
    // ... $this->page['data'] is the post
    $data = $this->page['data'];
    $siteName = $this->config['site_info']['name'];

    $this->setSiteinfo('json_ld', JsonLd::graph(
        JsonLd::article(array(
            'headline'      => $data['title'],
            'datePublished' => $data['post_date'],
            'dateModified'  => $data['modified'],
            // getImageUrl() includes the base path: only the domain is added
            'image'         => $data['image_id'] ? FEGG_CURRENT_DOMAIN . getImageUrl($data['image_id']) : '',
            'url'           => '/news/post_' . $data['news_id'] . '.html',
            'author'        => $siteName,
            'publisher'     => $siteName,
        )),
        JsonLd::breadcrumb(array(
            'ホーム'       => '/',
            'ニュース'     => '/news/',
            $data['title'] => '',
        ))
    ));

    $this->displayPage('news/detail');
}
```

The skeleton's siteframe writes it in the `<head>` of every page that sets it:

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/main/tpl/template/siteframe.tpl"
{{ if $site.json_ld }}
<script type="application/ld+json">{{ $site.json_ld|feggJs|noescape }}</script>
{{ end }}
```

[feggJs](../../basic/templates/security.mdx#javascript-feggjs) writes the array as JSON and encodes `<`, `>` and `&`, so a title containing `</script>` cannot end the element. Use exactly this line: `noescape` is required inside `<script>`, and the normal escaping would break the JSON.

In the skeleton, the top page (`Index.php`) sets the site and its organization, and the news detail page sets the article and its breadcrumbs.

## The data

- Empty values (`null`, `''`, `array()`) are left out, so optional fields can be passed as they come from the database.
- Paths (`url`, `item`, `image`, `logo`) become absolute URLs with `feggAbsoluteUrl()`: a path of the site without the base path (see [Absolute URLs](../sitemap/index.mdx#absolute-urls)).
- Dates (`'Y-m-d H:i:s'` from the database, a Unix time or a date object) are written in ISO 8601 with the time zone: `2026-11-15T10:00:00+09:00`.

```json title="Output (the article above, shortened)"
{
    "@context": "https://schema.org",
    "@graph": [
        {
            "@type": "NewsArticle",
            "headline": "New shop",
            "datePublished": "2026-11-15T10:00:00+09:00",
            "url": "https://www.example.com/news/post_12.html",
            "author": { "@type": "Organization", "name": "Example Inc." },
            "publisher": { "@type": "Organization", "name": "Example Inc." }
        },
        {
            "@type": "BreadcrumbList",
            "itemListElement": [
                { "@type": "ListItem", "position": 1, "name": "ホーム", "item": "https://www.example.com/" },
                { "@type": "ListItem", "position": 2, "name": "ニュース", "item": "https://www.example.com/news/" },
                { "@type": "ListItem", "position": 3, "name": "New shop" }
            ]
        }
    ]
}
```

Check a page with Google's [Rich Results Test](https://search.google.com/test/rich-results) after changing its data.

## Errors

- A date that cannot be read throws `\InvalidArgumentException`.

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/json-ld/methods/graph ---


# JsonLd::graph

> Put several items in one script

## Description

```php
public static array JsonLd::graph( array ...$items )
```

Returns `array('@context' => 'https://schema.org', '@graph' => array(...))` with the items in their order. The `@context` of each item is removed (the graph has it once), and empty items are left out.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| ...$items | array     |         | Results of the other methods, or arrays of your own with an `@type` |

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/src/Controller/Index.php"
use Fegg\Tool\JsonLd;

$siteName = $this->config['site_info']['name'];
$this->setSiteinfo('json_ld', JsonLd::graph(
    JsonLd::website($siteName, '/'),
    JsonLd::organization($siteName, '/')
));
```

An item of your own is a schema.org type as an array:

```php title="Controller"
$this->setSiteinfo('json_ld', JsonLd::graph(
    JsonLd::breadcrumb(array('ホーム' => '/', '店舗' => '/shops/', $shop['name'] => '')),
    array(
        '@type'     => 'Store',
        'name'      => $shop['name'],
        'telephone' => $shop['tel'],
        'url'       => feggAbsoluteUrl('/shops/' . $shop['shop_id'] . '/'),
    )
));
```

Arrays of your own are written as they are: make URLs absolute with `feggAbsoluteUrl()` yourself.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/json-ld/methods/organization ---


# JsonLd::organization

> The company or organization of the site

## Description

```php
public static array JsonLd::organization( string $name [, string $url = '/' [, string $logo = '' [, array $sameAs = array() ] ] ] )
```

Returns an `Organization` item. `$url` and `$logo` become absolute URLs; an empty `$logo` or `$sameAs` is left out.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default   | Remarks |
| ------- | --------- | --------- | ------- |
| $name   | string    |           | Name of the company or organization |
| $url    | string    | `'/'`     | Path or URL of its site |
| $logo   | string    | `''`      | Path or URL of the logo image |
| $sameAs | string[]  | `array()` | URLs of its other profiles (social accounts) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\JsonLd;

JsonLd::organization('Example Inc.', '/', '/data/img/common/logo.png', array(
    'https://www.instagram.com/example/',
));
```

```json title="Output"
{
    "@context": "https://schema.org",
    "@type": "Organization",
    "name": "Example Inc.",
    "url": "https://www.example.com/",
    "logo": "https://www.example.com/data/img/common/logo.png",
    "sameAs": ["https://www.instagram.com/example/"]
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/json-ld/methods/website ---


# JsonLd::website

> The site itself

## Description

```php
public static array JsonLd::website( string $name [, string $url = '/' ] )
```

Returns a `WebSite` item: the name search engines show for the site. Set it on the top page. `$url` becomes an absolute URL.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $name | string    |         | Name of the site |
| $url  | string    | `'/'`   | Path or URL of the top page |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\JsonLd;

JsonLd::website('Example Inc.');
```

```json title="Output"
{
    "@context": "https://schema.org",
    "@type": "WebSite",
    "name": "Example Inc.",
    "url": "https://www.example.com/"
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/json-ld/methods/breadcrumb ---


# JsonLd::breadcrumb

> Breadcrumbs of a page

## Description

```php
public static array JsonLd::breadcrumb( array $items )
```

Returns a `BreadcrumbList` item. `$items` is `name => path`, from the top page to the current page; the positions are numbered from 1. Paths become absolute URLs. The current page can have `''` as its path: it is then written without a URL.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $items | array     |         | `array('Name' => '/path/', ...)` in order |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\JsonLd;

JsonLd::breadcrumb(array(
    'ホーム'       => '/',
    'ニュース'     => '/news/',
    $data['title'] => '',
));
```

```json title="Output"
{
    "@context": "https://schema.org",
    "@type": "BreadcrumbList",
    "itemListElement": [
        { "@type": "ListItem", "position": 1, "name": "ホーム", "item": "https://www.example.com/" },
        { "@type": "ListItem", "position": 2, "name": "ニュース", "item": "https://www.example.com/news/" },
        { "@type": "ListItem", "position": 3, "name": "New shop" }
    ]
}
```

The names are array keys, so two items cannot have the same name. Write the same breadcrumbs as the page shows.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/json-ld/methods/article ---


# JsonLd::article

> An article: a news post, a blog post

## Description

```php
public static array JsonLd::article( array $data [, string $type = 'NewsArticle' ] )
```

Returns an article item. The keys below are converted; any other key is kept as it is. Empty values are left out.

Throws `\InvalidArgumentException` when a date cannot be read.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default         | Remarks |
| ----- | --------- | --------------- | ------- |
| $data | array     |                 | See below |
| $type | string    | `'NewsArticle'` | `'NewsArticle'`, `'BlogPosting'` or `'Article'` |

### Data

| Key           | Value | Written as |
| ------------- | ----- | ---------- |
| headline      | The title | As it is |
| datePublished | `'Y-m-d H:i:s'` from the database, a Unix time or a date object | ISO 8601 with the time zone |
| dateModified  | The same | ISO 8601 with the time zone |
| image         | A path or URL, or a list of them | A list of absolute URLs |
| url           | Path of the page | An absolute URL |
| author        | A name | `{"@type": "Organization", "name": ...}` (an array is kept as it is) |
| publisher     | A name | The same |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\JsonLd;

JsonLd::article(array(
    'headline'      => $data['title'],
    'datePublished' => $data['post_date'],
    'dateModified'  => $data['modified'],
    'image'         => $data['image_id'] ? FEGG_CURRENT_DOMAIN . getImageUrl($data['image_id']) : '',
    'url'           => '/blog/' . $data['blog_id'] . '/',
    'author'        => array('@type' => 'Person', 'name' => $data['writer']),
    'publisher'     => 'Example Inc.',
), 'BlogPosting');
```

`getImageUrl()` returns a path that already contains the base path, so only the domain is added (see [Absolute URLs](../../sitemap/index.mdx#absolute-urls)).


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/log ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Writing to the application log

`Fegg\Log` writes one line per entry to the application log, **`htdocs/_fegg/site/data/log/fegg-YYYY-MM-DD.log`** (one file a day). Call it from anywhere: controllers, your classes in `site/src/`, commands. Fegg writes uncaught errors there too (see [Logging and errors](../../basic/logging.mdx)).

## How to use

The methods are static: import `Fegg\Log` and call the method of the [level](#levels).

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Log;

Log::info('Import finished', array('rows' => $count));
Log::warning('Unknown category {id}', array('id' => $id));

try {
    $mail->send($to);
} catch (\Exception $e) {
    Log::error('Mail not sent to {to}', array('to' => $to, 'exception' => $e));
}
```

```text title="site/data/log/fegg-2026-11-14.log"
[2026-11-14 13:44:54 +09:00] INFO Import finished {"rows":120} | POST /cms/import/ 203.0.113.5
[2026-11-14 13:44:54 +09:00] WARNING Unknown category 7 {"id":7} | GET /news/?category=7 198.51.100.20
[2026-11-14 13:44:54 +09:00] ERROR Mail not sent to info@example.com {"to":"info@example.com","exception":"RuntimeException: SMTP connect() failed at /path/Contact.php:88"} | POST /contact/post/ 203.0.113.5
```

Each line is: the date and time, the level, the message, the context as JSON, and for web requests the method, the URL and the visitor's address.

- `{key}` in the message is replaced with `$context['key']` (text, numbers, `true` / `false` and objects with `__toString()`).
- The whole context is added as JSON. An exception (`\Throwable`) is written as its class, message, file and line; another object as its class name.
- Line breaks in the message become spaces, so one entry is always one line.
- Never log passwords, tokens or full personal data.

## Levels

The levels are those of PSR-3, from lowest to highest:

| Level       | Use for |
| :---------- | :------ |
| `debug`     | Details while developing |
| `info`      | Something worth knowing happened (an import finished) |
| `notice`    | Normal but significant |
| `warning`   | Something unexpected that the page could handle (another service did not answer) |
| `error`     | Something failed (a mail was not sent, a save failed) |
| `critical`  | A part of the site does not work |
| `alert`     | Someone must act now |
| `emergency` | The site is unusable |

Entries below `$settings['log_level']` are not written (see [Settings](#settings)).

## Settings

| Setting | Default | Remarks |
| :------ | :------ | :------ |
| `$settings['log_level']` | `''` | Lowest level written. `''`: `warning` in production, `debug` elsewhere |
| `$settings['log_retention_days']` | `30` | Files older than this are removed (checked once a day). `0`: keep all |
| `FEGG_LOG_DIR` (`env.php` or environment variable) | `site/data/log` | Directory of the log files (created when missing) |

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
$settings['log_level'] = 'info';
$settings['log_retention_days'] = 90;
```

`site/data/` is not tracked by git and is denied to the web by `htdocs/_fegg/.htaccess`. When the directory or the file cannot be written, the entry goes to the PHP error log instead.

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/log/methods/levels ---


# Log::error and the other levels

> Write an entry at a level

## Description

```php
public static bool Log::debug( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
public static bool Log::info( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
public static bool Log::notice( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
public static bool Log::warning( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
public static bool Log::error( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
public static bool Log::critical( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
public static bool Log::alert( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
public static bool Log::emergency( string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
```

Writes one line at the level of the method's name (see [Levels](../index.mdx#levels)). Returns `true` when the entry was written, `false` when it is below `$settings['log_level']` or could not be written (it then goes to the PHP error log).

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default   | Remarks |
| -------- | --------- | --------- | ------- |
| $message | string    |           | Message. `{key}` is replaced with `$context['key']` |
| $context | array     | `array()` | Values added to the line as JSON. Put an exception under `'exception'` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Log;

Log::info('{count} entries imported from {file}', array('count' => $count, 'file' => $name));

try {
    $mail->send($to);
} catch (\Exception $e) {
    Log::error('Mail not sent to {to}', array('to' => $to, 'exception' => $e));
    $this->page['error'] = true;
}
```

```text title="site/data/log/fegg-2026-11-14.log"
[2026-11-14 10:12:30 +09:00] INFO 120 entries imported from news.csv {"count":120,"file":"news.csv"} | POST /cms/import/ 203.0.113.5
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/log/methods/log ---


# Log::log

> Write an entry at a level given as text

## Description

```php
public static bool Log::log( string $level, string $message [, array $context = array() ] )
```

The same as [Log::error() and the other levels](./levels.mdx), with the level as an argument: for code that decides the level itself. An unknown level throws `\InvalidArgumentException`.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default   | Remarks |
| -------- | --------- | --------- | ------- |
| $level   | string    |           | `debug`, `info`, `notice`, `warning`, `error`, `critical`, `alert` or `emergency` (any case) |
| $message | string    |           | Message. `{key}` is replaced with `$context['key']` |
| $context | array     | `array()` | Values added to the line as JSON |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Log;

$level = $response['status'] >= 500 ? 'error' : 'warning';
Log::log($level, 'Items API answered {status}', array('status' => $response['status']));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/log/methods/setlogger ---


# Log::logger / Log::setLogger

> The logger in use, and replacing it in tests

## Description

```php
public static Fegg\Log\FileLogger Log::logger()
public static void Log::setLogger( ?Fegg\Log\FileLogger $logger )
```

`logger()` returns the `Fegg\Log\FileLogger` that writes the entries. It is created on first use from the [settings](../index.mdx#settings); its `file()` method returns the path of today's file.

`setLogger()` replaces it, for tests that read what was logged. `null` makes the next entry create it from the settings again.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type                    | Default | Remarks |
| ------- | ---------------------------- | ------- | ------- |
| $logger | Fegg\Log\FileLogger\|null    |         | `new FileLogger($directory, $lowestLevel = 'debug', $retentionDays = 30)`, or `null` |

## Example

```php title="Test"
use Fegg\Log;
use Fegg\Log\FileLogger;

$dir = sys_get_temp_dir() . '/fegg-log-test';
Log::setLogger(new FileLogger($dir, 'debug', 0));

$this->importer->run($file);

$this->assertStringContainsString('ERROR Import failed', file_get_contents(Log::logger()->file()));

Log::setLogger(null);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Sending mail (PHPMailer)

`Fegg\Tool\Mail` is a wrapper of the bundled [PHPMailer](https://github.com/PHPMailer/PHPMailer). Emails are sent as plain text, in UTF-8 with base64 encoding. By default they go through sendmail (the `sendmail_path` of php.ini is used when PHPMailer's default sendmail path does not exist); they can also be sent over [SMTP](#smtp).

## How to use

Import `Fegg\Tool\Mail` and create it with `new`. A failed send throws `PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception`, so wrap the sending in `try` / `catch`.

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Tool\Mail;
use PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception;

class Contact extends Controller
{
    public function post()
    {
        $body = $this->fetchPage('/contact/mail/to_admin');

        try {
            $mail = new Mail('Mail subject', $body);
            $mail->send('admin@example.com', 'Sender name', 'from@example.com');
        } catch (Exception $e) {
            // Failed to send: $e->getMessage()
        }
    }
}
```

The contact form of the skeleton (`htdocs/_fegg/site/src/Controller/Contact.php`) is a complete example, including [Mail::setDebugFlag](./methods/setdebugflag.mdx) to write the emails to a log file instead of sending them.

## Sender

When [Mail::send](./methods/send.mdx) is given no sender address, it uses `from` and `from_name` of `$settings['mail']` in `site/config/settings.php`. When there is no sender address in either place, `send()` throws an `\InvalidArgumentException`.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
$settings['mail'] = array('transport' => 'sendmail', 'from' => 'info@example.com', 'from_name' => 'Example Inc.');
```

```php title="Controller"
$mail = new Mail('Mail subject', $body);
$mail->send('admin@example.com');   // From: Example Inc. <info@example.com>
```

## SMTP

To send through an SMTP server instead of sendmail, set `$settings['mail']` in `site/config/settings.php`, and put the password in `site/config/env.php` (or an environment variable) as `FEGG_SMTP_PASSWORD`. Every `Mail` object then uses it, the contact form included.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
$settings['mail'] = array(
    'transport'  => 'smtp',
    'host'       => 'smtp.example.com',
    'port'       => 587,
    'encryption' => 'tls',
    'username'   => 'user@example.com',
);
```

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env.php"
'FEGG_SMTP_PASSWORD' => '...',
```

| Key          | Default       | Remarks |
| :----------- | :------------ | :------ |
| `transport`  | `'sendmail'`  | `'smtp'` to use SMTP |
| `host`       | `'localhost'` | SMTP server. `FEGG_SMTP_HOST` in `env.php` overrides it |
| `port`       | `587`         | Port |
| `encryption` | `'tls'`       | `'tls'`: STARTTLS. `'ssl'`: SMTPS (usually port 465). `''`: none |
| `username`   | `''`          | Login user; without it no authentication is used. `FEGG_SMTP_USERNAME` in `env.php` overrides it |
| `from`       | `''`          | Sender address when `send()` is given none (see [Sender](#sender)) |
| `from_name`  | `''`          | Sender name used with `from` |

The connection times out after 10 seconds. Never write the password in `settings.php`, which is committed.

:::note

The PHPMailer instance is available as `$mail->_mailer` when you need a setting this wrapper does not cover.

:::

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/construct ---


# Mail::\_\_construct

> Create a new email

## Description

```php
public Mail::__construct( [ string $subject = null, string $body = null, array $header = null ] )
```

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                    |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
| $subject | string    | null    | Email subject                                              |
| $body    | string    | null    | Email body (plain text)                                    |
| $header  | array     | null    | Custom headers, passed to [Mail::pushHeader](./pushheader.mdx) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');

// Same with
$mail = new Mail();
$mail->subject('Mail subject');
$mail->body('Mail body');
```

# Mail::subject

> Set mail subject

## Description

```php
public self Mail::subject( string $subject )
```

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks       |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | ------------- |
| $subject | string    |         | Email subject |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->subject('Mail subject');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/body ---


# Mail::body

> Set mail body

## Description

```php
public self Mail::body( string $body )
```

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                 |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------- |
| $body | string    |         | Email body (plain text) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->body('Mail body');

// Use a template for the body
$mail->body($this->fetchPage('/contact/mail/to_admin'));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/pushheader ---


# Mail::pushHeader

> Add custom header

## Description

```php
public self Mail::pushHeader( mixed $key, [ string $val = null ] )
```

Line breaks in the header name and value are removed.

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                                     |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| $key | mixed     |         | Header name, or an array of name => value   |
| $val | string    | null    | Header value when `$key` is a string        |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->pushHeader('X-Foo', 'bar');
$mail->pushHeader('X-Baz', 'qux');

// Same with
$mail->pushHeader(array(
    'X-Foo' => 'bar',
    'X-Baz' => 'qux',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/removeheader ---


# Mail::removeHeader

> Remove custom header

## Description

```php
public self Mail::removeHeader( [ string $key = null ] )
```

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $key | string    | null    | Header name / if omitted, all custom headers are removed |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->pushHeader('X-Foo', 'bar');
$mail->removeHeader('X-Foo');

// Remove all custom headers
$mail->removeHeader();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/setreplyto ---


# Mail::setReplyTo

> Add a Reply-To address

## Description

```php
public self Mail::setReplyTo( string $address, [ string $name = null ] )
```

Each call adds one more Reply-To address. Use [Mail::clearReplyTo](./clearreplyto.mdx) to remove them.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks          |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------- |
| $address | string    |         | Reply-To address |
| $name    | string    | null    | Reply-To name    |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
$mail->setReplyTo($this->in('mail'), $this->in('name'));
$mail->send('admin@example.com', 'Contact form', 'noreply@example.com');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/clearreplyto ---


# Mail::clearReplyTo

> Remove all Reply-To addresses

## Description

```php
public self Mail::clearReplyTo()
```

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->setReplyTo('foo@example.com');
$mail->clearReplyTo();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/pushattached ---


# Mail::pushAttached

> Add attachment file

## Description

```php
public self Mail::pushAttached( string $filePath, [ string $fileName = null ] )
```

Nothing is added when `$filePath` does not exist. Attachments are sent with [Mail::send](./send.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                     |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| $filePath | string    |         | Attachment file path                                        |
| $fileName | string    | null    | File name shown in the email / if omitted, the base name of `$filePath` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
$mail->pushAttached('path/to/file.pdf', 'document.pdf');
$mail->send('mail@example.com', 'John Doe', 'from@example.com');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/send ---


# Mail::send

> Send email

## Description

```php
public boolean Mail::send( mixed $to, [ string $from_name = null, string $from_ad = null ] )
```

Sends the email with its attachments and returns `true` on success. A failed send (including an invalid address) throws `PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception`.

No sender (`$from_ad` `null` or `''`): `$settings['mail']` (see [Sender](../index.mdx#sender)); none there throws `\InvalidArgumentException`.

When the debug flag is enabled with [Mail::setDebugFlag](./setdebugflag.mdx), the email is not sent: the whole message is written to the log file with [Mail::log](./log.mdx) instead.

:::note

Each call sends only to the addresses given to it, so one `Mail` object can send the same message to several people in turn. To send one email to several recipients, pass them as an array.

:::

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks                                         |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| $to        | mixed     |         | Recipient address, or an array of addresses     |
| $from_name | string    | null    | Sender name (none: `$settings['mail']['from_name']` when `$from_ad` is not given either) |
| $from_ad   | string    | null    | Sender address, also used as the envelope sender (none: `$settings['mail']['from']`) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;
use PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception;

try {
    $mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
    $mail->send('mail@example.com', 'John Doe', 'from@example.com');

    // Several recipients
    $mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
    $mail->send(array('foo@example.com', 'bar@example.com'), 'John Doe', 'from@example.com');

    // The sender of $settings['mail']
    $mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
    $mail->send('mail@example.com');
} catch (Exception $e) {
    // Failed to send: $e->getMessage()
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/log ---


# Mail::log

> Append a message to the log file

## Description

```php
public boolean Mail::log( string $message )
```

Appends a separator line and `$message` to the log file ([Mail::logFile](./logfile.mdx): the file given to [Mail::setDebugFlag](./setdebugflag.mdx), by default `mail.log` in the log directory). Nothing is written when the file or its directory is not writable. The return value is always `true`.

[Mail::send](./send.mdx) calls this method with the whole message when the debug flag is enabled.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | ----------- |
| $message | string    |         | Log message |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->setDebugFlag(true);
$mail->log('Contact form posted');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/setdebugflag ---


# Mail::setDebugFlag

> Set debug flag and log file name

## Description

```php
public self Mail::setDebugFlag( boolean $is_debug, [ string $log_name = null ] )
```

When `$is_debug` is `true`, [Mail::send](./send.mdx) does not send the email but writes the whole message to the log file: `$log_name`, or `mail.log` in the log directory when it is not given (`htdocs/_fegg/site/data/log/mail.log`, or the directory of `FEGG_LOG_DIR`). [Mail::logFile](./logfile.mdx) returns the file used.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                    |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------- |
| $is_debug | boolean   |         | `true`: write a log instead of sending |
| $log_name | string    | null    | Log file path (none: `mail.log` in the log directory) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
$mail->setDebugFlag(true);                                                // site/data/log/mail.log
$mail->setDebugFlag(true, FEGG_SITE_DIR . '/data/log/contact_admin.log'); // another file

// Written to the log file instead of being sent
$mail->send('mail@example.com', 'John Doe', 'from@example.com');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/logfile ---


# Mail::logFile

> Get the path of the debug log file

## Description

```php
public string Mail::logFile()
```

Returns the file [Mail::log](./log.mdx) writes to: the file given to [Mail::setDebugFlag](./setdebugflag.mdx), or `mail.log` in the log directory (`htdocs/_fegg/site/data/log`, or `FEGG_LOG_DIR` when it is set).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->setDebugFlag(true);
echo $mail->logFile(); // -> ".../htdocs/_fegg/site/data/log/mail.log"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Read and write database tables with `?` placeholders

`Fegg\Model` builds SQL with `?` placeholders on top of [Fegg\Database\Pdo](../database/index.mdx). It is the normal way for a controller to read and write the database. SQLite is the default; the connection is set in `site/config/database_config.php` (see [Configuration](../database/configuration.mdx)).

## How to use

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Model;

class News extends Controller
{
    public function __init()
    {
        parent::__init();

        $this->_model = new Model(false, array(
            'table'   => 'cms_news',
            'id'      => 'news_id',
            'visible' => 'visible',
        ));
    }

    public function index()
    {
        $this->page['data'] = $this->_model
            ->where('category = ?', 2)
            ->order('post_date', 'desc')
            ->listMax(20)
            ->current($this->in('p'))
            ->find();
        $this->page['pager'] = $this->_model->getPager();

        $this->displayPage('news');
    }
}
```

## Tables

Tables are defined in YAML: `htdocs/_fegg/site/config/database/*.yaml` (project) and `htdocs/_fegg/core/config/database/*.yaml` (CMS core tables). `./fegg-cli migration` applies them to the database.

Use `./fegg-cli db:schema` to see the tables and columns (`./fegg-cli db:schema cms_news` for one table, `--json` for JSON). It reads the YAML files, so no database is needed, and it warns about tables that lack the columns below.

### Required columns

Every table used with `Fegg\Model` needs these columns. The model fills them by itself (see `core/config/database_regular_use_query.php`):

| Column     | Type       | Set by                                                                                         |
| ---------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `valid`    | `BOOLEAN`  | `1` on insert, `0` by [delete](./methods/delete.mdx). Reads only return rows with `valid = 1` |
| `created`  | `DATETIME` | insert                                                                                         |
| `modified` | `DATETIME` | insert and update                                                                              |

## Creating a model

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',      // table name
    'id'      => 'news_id',       // primary key
    'visible' => 'visible',       // optional: public flag column
    'bind'    => array('category' => 'INTEGER', 'post_date' => 'DATETIME'), // casts on write
));
```

- The first argument is `$is_admin`. With `false`, reads only return rows whose `visible` column is 1 (public pages). The CMS uses `true` to see unpublished rows. See [Model::\_\_construct](./methods/construct.mdx).
- `bind` types are `INTEGER`, `FLOAT`, `DATE` and `DATETIME` (case-insensitive). Any other type throws `InvalidArgumentException`. See [Model::addBindParam](./methods/addbindparam.mdx).
- Table and column names (`table`, `id`, `visible`, and the names given to `order()`, `join()`, `whereIn()` and the other helpers) must be plain identifiers: letters, digits and `_`, optionally `table.column` (`news_id`, `cms_news.post_date`). Anything else throws `InvalidArgumentException`. Never build names from user input.

## Reading

```php title="Controller"
$rows = $model
    ->where('category = ?', 2)
    ->whereLike('title', 'news')                 // contains; % and _ match literally
    ->whereIn('news_id', array(1, 2, 3))
    ->order('post_date', 'desc')
    ->listMax(20)->current($this->in('p'))       // page size and page number
    ->find();                                    // array of rows (associative arrays)
$pager = $model->getPager();                     // current_page, last_page, number_of_records ...

$rows  = $model->order('post_date', 'desc')->limit(5)->all();      // no paging
$row   = $model->id($id);                                          // one row by primary key, or null
$row   = $model->where('title = ?', $title)->one();                // first match, or null
$title = $model->where('news_id = ?', $id)->value('title');        // one value, or null
$ids   = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->column('news_id');      // array(3, 5, ...)
$count = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->count();                // int
$found = $model->where('title = ?', $title)->exists();             // bool
```

| Method                                            | Returns                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [find](./methods/find.mdx)                        | One page of rows; [getPager](./methods/getpager.mdx) has the links     |
| [all](./methods/all.mdx)                          | Every matching row ([limit](./methods/limit.mdx) caps it)              |
| [one](./methods/one.mdx) / [id](./methods/id.mdx) | The first row, or `null`                                               |
| [value](./methods/value.mdx)                      | The first column of the first row, or `null`                           |
| [column](./methods/column.mdx)                    | One column of every row, optionally keyed by another column            |
| [count](./methods/count.mdx)                      | Number of rows (of groups, with `group()`)                             |
| [exists](./methods/exists.mdx)                    | `true` when a row matches                                              |
| [toSql](./methods/tosql.mdx)                      | The SELECT `all()` would run, with the values filled in; runs nothing  |

### find() or all()

`find()` pages: set the page size with [listMax](./methods/listmax.mdx) and the page with [current](./methods/current.mdx), then read the links with `getPager()`. `all()` does not page; [limit](./methods/limit.mdx) caps it. Do not use `listMax(9999)` to get everything, and do not combine `limit()` with `find()` (it throws `LogicException`).

### Conditions are reset after every query

`where()`, `order()`, `select()`, `limit()` and the other chain methods only apply to the next query. After every query (`find()`, `all()`, `one()`, `count()`, `insertRow()` ...), failed ones included, the model forgets them, so each query starts clean. [Model::reset](./methods/reset.mdx) does the same by hand.

The write methods ([updateRowsWhere](./methods/updaterowswhere.mdx), [deleteWhere](./methods/deletewhere.mdx) ...) take their condition as arguments. Conditions chained with `where()` are not used by them (and are cleared).

## Writing

```php title="Controller"
$id  = $model->insertRow(array('title' => $title, 'category' => 2, 'content' => null));
$n   = $model->updateRow(array('title' => $title), $id);                 // rows updated
$n   = $model->updateRowsWhere(array('visible' => 0), 'category = ?', 3);
$ids = $model->insertRows(array($row1, $row2));      // one transaction: all rows or none
$n   = $model->delete($id);                          // soft delete: valid = 0
$n   = $model->erase($id);                           // removes the row
```

- [insertRow](./methods/insertrow.mdx), [updateRow](./methods/updaterow.mdx) and [updateRowsWhere](./methods/updaterowswhere.mdx) write only the given keys; `null` is stored as NULL. Do not pass `created`, `modified` or `valid` unless you mean to override them.
- Update and delete methods return the number of affected rows.
- [delete](./methods/delete.mdx) and [deleteWhere](./methods/deletewhere.mdx) are soft deletes (`valid = 0`; reads no longer see the row). [erase](./methods/erase.mdx) and [eraseWhere](./methods/erasewhere.mdx) remove the row.
- `updateWhere()`, `deleteWhere()`, `eraseWhere()` and `updateRowsWhere()` refuse an empty condition. Pass `'1 = 1'` to really affect every row.
- [insert](./methods/insert.mdx) and [update](./methods/update.mdx) write `''` for every listed column missing from the data and cannot store NULL: prefer `insertRow()` and `updateRow()`.
- [transaction](./methods/transaction.mdx) runs several writes, of any number of models, all or nothing.

## Errors

A failed query or connection throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException` (see [Database](../database/index.mdx#errors)). Catch it only when the page can do something useful, for example with a duplicate key:

```php title="Controller"
try {
    $model->insertRow($row);
} catch (\Fegg\Database\QueryException $e) {
    // ...
}
```

Wrong use of the builder (an array bound to `<`, an invalid column name, an update without a condition) throws `InvalidArgumentException` with a message saying what to do instead.

## Debugging

[toSql](./methods/tosql.mdx) returns the SELECT a chain would run, without running it. [debug](./methods/debug.mdx) prints the last query. Both fill in the values for reading.

```php title="Controller"
echo $model->where('category = ?', 2)->order('post_date', 'desc')->toSql();
// Select * From "cms_news" Where category = 2 AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1 Order By post_date DESC
```

## Raw SQL

For what the model cannot express, use [Fegg\Database\Pdo](../database/index.mdx) directly. It shares the connection (and an open transaction) with the models. Raw queries get no automatic `valid` / `created` / `modified` handling.

## Methods

--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/construct ---


# Model::\_\_construct

> Model class constructor

## Description

```php
public Model::__construct( [ boolean $is_admin = false, array $options = [] ] )
```

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `table`, `id` or `visible` is not a plain name, or when a `bind` type is unknown.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                                                             |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| $is_admin | boolean   | false   | `false`: reads only return rows whose `visible` column is 1 (public pages). `true`: every row (CMS) |
| $options  | array     | []      | Model options (below)                                                                               |

### Options

| Key       | Data type | Remarks                                                                                           |
| --------- | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `table`   | string    | Table name. Same as [setTableName](./settablename.mdx)                                            |
| `id`      | string    | Primary key column. Same as [setPrimary](./setprimary.mdx)                                        |
| `visible` | string    | Public flag column (optional). Same as [setVisible](./setvisible.mdx)                             |
| `bind`    | array     | Column types cast on write (`column => type`). Same as [setBindParam](./setbindparam.mdx)         |

`table`, `id` and `visible` must be plain names: letters, digits and `_`, optionally `table.column`. Other keys are ignored.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Model;

// For public pages (rows whose visible column is 0 are not returned)
$model = new Model(false, array(
    // Operate on the `cms_news` table
    'table'   => 'cms_news',

    // Primary key is `news_id`
    'id'      => 'news_id',

    // Public flag is `visible`
    'visible' => 'visible',

    // Cast these columns on insert and update
    'bind'    => array(
        'category'  => 'INTEGER',
        'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
        'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
        'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
    ),
));

// For CMS pages (every row, visible or not)
$admin = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/settablename ---


# Model::setTableName

> Set Table Name

## Description

```php
public self Model::setTableName( string $table )
```

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$table` is not a plain name (letters, digits and `_`).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks           |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ----------------- |
| $table | string    |         | Target table name |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->setTableName('cms_news');

// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table' => 'cms_news',
));
```

```php
public string Model::getTableName()
```

## Example

```php title="Controller"

    'table' => 'cms_news',
));

echo $model->getTableName();
// -> 'cms_news'
```

# Model::setPrimary


| ---- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------- |
| $id  | string    |         | Primary key column name |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'id' => 'news_id',
));
```

# Model::setVisible

> Set Visible flag name

## Description

```php
public self Model::setVisible( string|null $visible )
```

When the model was created with `$is_admin = false`, reads only return rows whose visible column is 1. `null` or `''` removes the flag. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$visible` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type   | Default | Remarks                  |
| -------- | ----------- | ------- | ------------------------ |
| $visible | string/null |         | Public flag column name  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"

$model->setVisible('visible');

// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'visible' => 'visible',
));
```

# Model::setBindParam

> Set Bind Parameter types

## Description

```php
public self Model::setBindParam( array $bind )
```

Replaces every bind type set before. See [addBindParam](./addbindparam.mdx) for the types and what they do.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                         |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |
| $bind | array     |         | Bind types (`column => type`)   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->setBindParam(array(
    'category'  => 'INTEGER',
    'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
    'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
    'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
));

// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'bind' => array(
        'category'  => 'INTEGER',
        'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
        'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
        'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
    ),
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/addbindparam ---


# Model::addBindParam

> Add Bind Parameter types

## Description

```php
public self Model::addBindParam( string $column, string $type )
```

The value of the column is cast to the type before it is written by [insertRow](./insertrow.mdx), [insertRows](./insertrows.mdx), [updateRow](./updaterow.mdx), [updateRowsWhere](./updaterowswhere.mdx), [insert](./insert.mdx), [update](./update.mdx) and [updateWhere](./updatewhere.mdx). `null` values are not cast. Reads are not affected.

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` for an unknown type.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                          |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name                      |
| $type   | string    |         | Bind type (case-insensitive)     |

### Types

| Type       | Cast                                                                                    |
| ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `INTEGER`  | `(int)`                                                                                 |
| `FLOAT`    | `(float)`                                                                               |
| `DATE`     | `Y-m-d`. A number is read as a Unix timestamp, anything else with `strtotime()`         |
| `DATETIME` | `Y-m-d H:i:s`. A number is read as a Unix timestamp, anything else with `strtotime()`   |

A `DATE` / `DATETIME` string that `strtotime()` cannot read is stored as `1970-01-01`; validate dates before writing them.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->addBindParam('category', 'INTEGER');
$model->addBindParam('image_id', 'INTEGER');
$model->addBindParam('file_id', 'INTEGER');
$model->addBindParam('post_date', 'DATETIME');

// Same as the following
$model = new Model();
$model->setBindParam(array(
    'category'  => 'INTEGER',
    'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
    'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
    'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/getbindparam ---


# Model::getBindParam

> Get Bind Parameter types

## Description

```php
public array Model::getBindParam()
```

The types are returned in upper case.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'bind' => array(
        'category'  => 'integer',
        'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
    ),
));

var_dump($model->getBindParam());
```

```php title="Output"
array(2) {
  ["category"]=> string(7) "INTEGER"
  ["post_date"]=> string(8) "DATETIME"
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/select ---


# Model::select

> Set Select Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::select( string $select )
```

Sets the columns of the next SELECT (default `*`). The text is written into the SQL as it is: never put user input in it. Like every chain method, it is reset after the query.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                     |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| $select | string    |         | Columns or expressions, separated by commas |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Get the `news_id` and `title` columns
$records = $model->select('news_id, title')->find();

// Expressions work too
$records = $model->select('category, count(*) AS n')->group('category')->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/where ---


# Model::where

> Set Where Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::where( string $query, [ mixed $data = [], string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds a condition with `?` placeholders. Values are always bound, never written into the SQL. Several calls are joined with `AND` (or `OR` with `$andOr`).

- A query without placeholders (`'note IS NULL'`) needs no `$data`.
- An array bound to one placeholder expands it (`id IN (?, ?, ...)`), see [Values and placeholders](../../database/index.mdx#values-and-placeholders). An array that cannot be expanded throws when the query runs.
- An empty `$query` (`''`) adds nothing, which is handy for optional search conditions.

A query with its own `OR` outside brackets is put in brackets when it is joined to other conditions: `where('category = ?', 1)->where('title = ? OR summary = ?', array($q, $q))` is `category = 1 AND (title = ? OR summary = ?)`. The conditions the model adds by itself (the visible flag and `valid = 1`) are joined with `AND` after yours, with yours in brackets when they have an `OR` (`Where (a = ? OR b = ?) AND ...`), so hidden and deleted rows never match. [having](./having.mdx) works the same way. To group conditions inside your own, use [whereOpen](./whereopen.mdx) and [whereClose](./whereclose.mdx).

For common cases there are helpers that also check the column name: [whereIn](./wherein.mdx), [whereNotIn](./wherenotin.mdx), [whereLike](./wherelike.mdx), [whereNull](./wherenull.mdx), [whereNotNull](./wherenotnull.mdx) and [whereBetween](./wherebetween.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                           |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| $query | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders (never put user input in it)      |
| $data  | mixed     | []      | One value, or an array of values in placeholder order             |
| $andOr | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`                |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$records = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->find();
// Select * From "cms_news" Where category = 2 AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1 ...

// Several placeholders: an array in placeholder order
$records = $model->where('category = ? AND post_date >= ?', array(2, '2026-01-01'))->find();

// An array on one placeholder: IN (...)
$records = $model->where('news_id IN (?)', array(1, 2, 3))->find();
// ... Where news_id IN (1, 2, 3) AND ...
```

### Complex WHERE query

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 2);
$model->whereOpen();
$model->where('title = ?', 'foo');
$model->where('post_date <= ?', '2021-01-01', 'OR');
$model->whereClose();

// Select *
// From "cms_news"
// Where category = 2
//   AND (title = 'foo' OR post_date <= '2021-01-01')
//   AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1
$records = $model->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/whereopen ---


# Model::whereOpen

> Add open bracket on Where query

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereOpen( [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Opens a bracket in the WHERE clause; close it with [whereClose](./whereclose.mdx). The first condition inside the bracket is not prefixed with `AND` / `OR`.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                                            |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| $andOr | string    | 'AND'   | How the bracket joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 2);
$model->whereOpen();
$model->where('title = ?', 'foo');
$model->where('post_date <= ?', '2021-01-01', 'OR');
$model->whereClose();

// Select *
// From "cms_news"
// Where category = 2
//   AND (title = 'foo' OR post_date <= '2021-01-01')
//   AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1
$records = $model->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/whereclose ---


# Model::whereClose

> Add close bracket on Where query

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereClose()
```

Closes the bracket opened with [whereOpen](./whereopen.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 2);
$model->whereOpen();
$model->where('title = ?', 'foo');
$model->where('post_date <= ?', '2021-01-01', 'OR');
$model->whereClose();

// Select *
// From "cms_news"
// Where category = 2
//   AND (title = 'foo' OR post_date <= '2021-01-01')
//   AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1
$records = $model->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherein ---


# Model::whereIn

> Match a column against a list of values

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereIn( string $column, array $values, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column IN (?, ?, ...)`. An empty array matches no row (`1 = 0`), so an empty selection returns nothing instead of failing. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name (`news_id` or `cms_news.news_id`)        |
| $values | array     |         | Values to match                                      |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Where news_id IN (1, 2, 3) AND ...
$records = $model->whereIn('news_id', array(1, 2, 3))->all();

// ... Where 1 = 0 AND ...  (no rows)
$records = $model->whereIn('news_id', array())->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherenotin ---


# Model::whereNotIn

> Exclude a list of values

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereNotIn( string $column, array $values, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column NOT IN (?, ?, ...)`. An empty array excludes nothing (`1 = 1`). Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name (`news_id` or `cms_news.news_id`)        |
| $values | array     |         | Values to exclude                                    |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Other news than the current one
// ... Where news_id NOT IN (5) AND ...
$records = $model->whereNotIn('news_id', array(5))->order('post_date', 'desc')->limit(3)->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherelike ---


# Model::whereLike

> Search a column for a term

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereLike( string $column, string $term, [ string $mode = 'contains', string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column LIKE ? ESCAPE '!'`. `%` and `_` in `$term` match literally, so user input can be passed as it is. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name or `$mode` is unknown.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default    | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |            | Column name                                          |
| $term   | string    |            | Text to search for                                   |
| $mode   | string    | 'contains' | Where the term must be (below)                       |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'      | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

### Modes

| Mode       | Pattern    |
| ---------- | ---------- |
| `contains` | `%term%`   |
| `prefix`   | `term%`    |
| `suffix`   | `%term`    |
| `exact`    | `term`     |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Where title LIKE '%50!%%' ESCAPE '!' AND ...  (titles containing "50%")
$records = $model->whereLike('title', '50%')->find();

// Titles starting with "News"
$records = $model->whereLike('title', 'News', 'prefix')->find();

// Search title or content
$keyword = $this->in('keyword');
$records = $model
    ->whereOpen()
    ->whereLike('title', $keyword)
    ->whereLike('content', $keyword, 'contains', 'OR')
    ->whereClose()
    ->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherenull ---


# Model::whereNull

> Match rows where a column is NULL

```php
public self Model::whereNull( string $column, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column IS NULL`. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |

| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Where image_id IS NULL AND ...
$records = $model->whereNull('image_id')->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherenotnull ---

> Match rows where a column is not NULL

```php
public self Model::whereNotNull( string $column, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column IS NOT NULL`. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',

```
